## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
The mission is AGGREGATION - robots need to get into the BLUE-bordered strip (black floor). Score increases per robot outside the target region per step, so we want ALL robots in the black-floored strip near blue walls as fast as possible.

**Key observations:**
- Target region has BLACK floor and BLUE walls
- The champion uses: Spiral → Exploration → GoToColor(blue/clr3) → Attraction
- The GoToColor with clr3 (blue) makes sense - move toward blue walls
- Attraction keeps robots together once they arrive
- The champion score (10164) is much better than others (~20000+)

**Strategy:**

1. **Survival**: Copy champion exactly
2. **Exploitation**: Small tweaks to champion's parameters
   - Adjust spiral speed, exploration turn rate, attraction gain, probabilities
3. **Exploration**: Try different topologies
   - GoToColor(blue) as primary with floor-based transitions
   - Direct approach: explore until see blue, go to blue, stop/attract on black floor

Let me design the machines:

**Champion analysis:**
- S0: Spiral(9) → black floor (→S1), gray floor (→S1 or S0)
- S1: Exploration(41) → white floor, gray floor(→S3), neighbor count(→S2), color perception(→S0)
- S2: GoToColor(blue/clr3) → gray(→S1), random(→S3)
- S3: Attraction(2.866) → few neighbors(→S3 self, wait... →S3 is same state, but D=3 from state1 means... with 4 states, from state 1, others are [0,2,3], D=3 means state 3)

The champion's transition n3x0: from S3, D=3 means... with 4 states leaving state 3, others are [0,1,2], D=3 would be out of range. Wait: D=3 from state 3 - others are [0,1,2], so D=3 is invalid... but it's the champion. Let me re-read: "With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2." So with 4 states, leaving state 3, the others are [0,1,2], indexed 0,1,2. So D=3 from state 3 would be state... hmm. Actually D is an index into the list of OTHER states. From state 3 with 4 states total, others are [0,1,2], so max D is 2. But champion has D=3... Maybe it wraps or it's the champion as-is. I'll just copy it verbatim.

## Reasoning

The champion (score 8379.78) improved significantly from round 1 (10164.71). The top 5 performers all share the same basic topology:
- **S0**: Spiral → transitions on black floor, gray floor (twice)
- **S1**: Exploration → transitions on white floor, gray floor, neighbor count (above), color perception (blue/clr3)
- **S2**: GoToColor(blue/clr3) → transitions on gray floor, random probability
- **S3**: Attraction → transition on few neighbors (below threshold)

Key differences between top performers:
- Champion has `--n1x1 2` (→S2) vs original which had `--n1x1 3` (→S3) - this is a critical difference!
- Spiral speed: 7-12 range works well
- Attraction gain: 2.5-3.2 range
- The extra transition in S2 (n2x2 for black floor) in performer #2 is interesting

**Strategy:**
1. Copy champion exactly (survival)
2. Exploit top performers: vary spiral speed (6-15), attraction gain (2.0-4.0), exploration rate, probabilities
3. Try adding a transition from S3 back to S2 (GoToColor) when on gray floor - robots that drift back should re-navigate
4. Try increasing the gray→S2 transition probability in S1
5. Explore: add neighbor-based transitions, try different floor-condition probabilities

## Reasoning

**Big breakthrough!** The new champion (6215.29) is a 3-state machine, much simpler than the 4-state versions:
- **S0**: Spiral(9) → black floor (→S1, p=0.05), gray floor (→S1, p=0.5)
- **S1**: GoToColor(blue/clr3) → gray floor (→S0, p=0.08), black floor (→S0(?), wait... n1x1 dest=1 means from state 1 the others are [0,2], D=1 means S2), neighbor count above 8 (→S1, D=1 means S2 with w=1)
- **S2**: Attraction(2.866) → few neighbors below 5 (→S1, D=1 means the other non-S2, which with 3 states leaving S2, others are [0,1], D=1=S1), gray floor (→S1, p=0.2)

Wait, let me re-read the champion carefully:
- S0: Spiral, transitions: n0x0→D=1(S1) on black floor p=0.05; n0x1→D=1(S1) on gray floor p=0.5
- S1: GoToColor(blue clr3), transitions: n1x0→D=0(S0) on gray floor p=0.08; n1x1→D=1(S2) on black floor p=0.9; n1x2→D=1(S2) on neighbor>8 w=1
- S2: Attraction, transitions: n2x0→D=1(S1) on few neighbors<5 w=17; n2x1→D=1(S1) on gray floor p=0.2

So the logic is: Spiral to explore → GoToBlue when on black or gray floor → once on black with neighbors or many neighbors, Attract → return to GoToBlue if isolated or on gray.

**Strategy:**
1. Copy champion exactly
2. Exploit: vary spiral speed (7,8,10,11), GoToColor probabilities, attraction gain, neighbor thresholds
3. Add 4th state for more complex behavior
4. Try different black floor probabilities in S0 transitions
5. Try varying the gray→S0 probability in S1 (currently 0.08, maybe lower keeps robots moving toward blue longer)
6. Try adding color perception transition in S1

## Reasoning

- S0: Spiral(9), transitions: black floor→S1 (p=0.05), gray floor→S1 (p=0.5)
- S1: GoToColor(blue/clr3), transitions: gray→S0 (p=0.03), black→S2 (p=0.9), neighbors>6 w=2→S2
- S2: Attraction(2.866), transitions: neighbors<5 w=17→S1, gray→S1 (p=0.15)

**Key insights from rounds 5-6:**
- The champion improved by: lower gray-return p (0.03 vs 0.05), lower neighbor threshold (6 vs 7), higher steepness (2.0 vs 1.5)
- Trend: lower p1x0 (gray→S0 in S1) is better: 0.08→0.05→0.03
- Neighbor threshold 6 with w=2 beats 7 with w=1.5 and 8 with w=2
- p1x1 (black→S2) = 0.9 consistently best
- w2x0=17 with p2x0=5 for neighbor-below transition seems optimal; p2x0=6/w=16 is worse
- Worst: neighbor threshold 8 w=2, adding extra transitions to S2

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Major breakthrough!** The new champion is a 2-state machine (4413.61):
- S0: GoToColor(blue/clr3) → black floor (→S1, p=0.95), neighbors>6 w=2 (→S1)
- S1: Attraction(2.866) → neighbors<5 w=17 (→S0), gray floor (→S0, p=0.15)

This is much simpler - no spiral state needed! Robots go directly toward blue walls, switch to attraction when on black floor or when grouped, then return to GoToBlue when isolated or on gray.

**Key observations:**
- 2-state is better than 3-state (removes the spiral overhead)
- The neighbor>6 w=2 transition in S0 is crucial - groups start attracting early
- p=0.95 for black floor→attract is high, keeping robots in attraction state on black floor
- att=2.866 and neighbor<5 w=17 for returning to GoToBlue seem well-tuned

**Exploitation targets:**
1. Vary p0x0 (black→S1): 0.9, 0.92, 0.97, 0.99
2. Vary neighbor threshold p0x1: 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 with w=2
3. Vary steepness w0x1: 1.5, 2.5, 3.0
4. Vary att1: 2.0, 2.5, 3.0, 3.5
5. Vary p1x0 (neighbor<5) threshold: 4, 6 and w1x0: 15, 18, 20
6. Vary p1x1 (gray→S0): 0.1, 0.12, 0.2

**Exploration:**
- Try adding a 3rd transition to S0 (gray floor) or S1
- Try 3-state with GoToColor as first state (no spiral)

## Reasoning

**Champion analysis (4319.50):**
- S0: GoToColor(blue/clr3) → black floor→S1 (p=0.95), neighbors>6 w=2→S1
- S1: Attraction(3.0) → neighbors<5 w=17→S0, gray→S0 (p=0.15)

Key improvement from previous: att=3.0 (vs 2.866). The 2-state topology is clearly winning.

**Gradient from best performers:**
- att=3.0 > 2.866 > 2.5 (monotone - try 3.2, 3.5, 4.0)
- Neighbor threshold 6 w=2 ≈ 5 w=2.5 ≈ 7 w=2 (all competitive)
- p0x0=0.95 is good; 0.97 slightly worse
- p1x1=0.15 best; 0.1 worse
- 3-state version performs much worse

**Exploitation targets:**
1. Try att=3.2, 3.5, 3.8, 4.0 (pushing attraction gain up)
2. Try neighbor threshold combinations: 4 w=3.0, 5 w=2.0, 6 w=2.5
3. Try p1x0 threshold variations: 4, 6 with different steepness
4. Try p1x1 (gray→S0): 0.12, 0.18
5. Try p0x0 (black→S1): 0.93, 0.98, 0.99
6. Add a third transition to S0 (gray floor→S1) to catch robots on gray that have neighbors
7. Try att=3.0 with varied neighbor threshold (5 w=2.5)

**Key insight:** att=3.0 with neighbor threshold 5 w=2.5 might combine the best of performers 1 and 2.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Champion analysis (3876.26):**
- S0: GoToColor(blue/clr3) → black floor→S1 (p=0.95), neighbors>6 w=2→S1
- S1: Attraction(3.0) → neighbors<4 w=18→S0, gray→S0 (p=0.15)

Key improvement: neighbor-below threshold dropped from 5 to **4** with steepness 18 (vs 17). This means robots only return to GoToBlue when very isolated (fewer than 4 neighbors). Combined with att=3.0.

**Gradient from best performers:**
- Performer 2 (3882.36): same but p1x0=5/w=17 and p1x1=0.18 - nearly identical
- att=3.0, 3.2, 3.5 all give ~4023 (att=3.0 may be slightly better with threshold 4)
- Worst: p1x1=0.12 worse than 0.15; threshold 7 w=2 worse; adding gray transition to S0 badly hurts

**What to exploit:**
1. Combine champion params with p1x1=0.18 (performer 2's advantage)
2. Try threshold 3 w=20 (even stricter - stay in attraction unless very alone)
3. Try threshold 4 w=20 (sharper sigmoid)
4. Try p0x0=0.97, 0.99 (keep more robots on GoToBlue→Attract faster)
5. Try att=3.0 with threshold 4 w=19, 20
6. Try threshold 3 w=18 
7. Try p1x1=0.1, 0.2 to bracket the optimum
8. Try adding neighbor-above transition to S1 (reinforce attraction when many neighbors)

**Final round strategy:** concentrate on the proven 2-state topology, fine-tune threshold/steepness and p1x1 around the champion.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 21 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.54 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
